#2882. Player Grades: Dallas gets on track against the Jazz with a 112-100 win

The Dallas Mavericks (5-4) took down the Utah Jazz (1-8), 112-100 on Friday night. The victory gets Dallas back on the right track after two-straight losses and gives the Mavs a 2-0 record in Emirates Cup play. Cooper Flagg and Kyrie Irving struggled a bit in the early going, but locked in down the stretch, while Morez Johnson, Jr. had the best game of his nascent NBA career.

We're switching it up this time and using a numerical grading system on a 10-point scale to grade the players. Let's get to the grades!

Irving was not sharp from the outset and had a difficult shooting night (7-of-20 overall; 0-of-3 from downtown). While that pulled down his grade, his all-out effort and hustle can't be overlooked. All night, Irving was on the floor lunging for steals, going amongst the trees for a couple of boards and hitting a few key shots in the latter part of the game to keep the Jazz at bay. It was not his best shooting night, but Irving made up for it in other ways, particularly on the defensive end.

Flagg shook off the massive funk he was in during the prior two games, but it took him until the second half to do so. He was 7-of-16 from the floor, with most of the misses coming in the first half and got completely lost on a couple of defensive sets, but he persisted. When it was all said and done, he nearly put up a triple-double. Three turnovers and 1-of-4 shooting from deep didn't help his grade, but this is the type of game that reminds you how special he is. He can have a rough half, yet end with a box score that looks great while having a true positive effect on the outcome of the game.

We try to show courtesy to all starters by featuring them in our Grades coverage, but Martin simply can't be graded. He missed a three-point attempt, committed two fouls and that's about it. He gets saved from an apparent 1/10 grade because he played so few minutes. Martin has been fine this year and contributes in ways that don't necessarily show in the box score, but one has to wonder if and when Zaccharie Risacher or Naji Marshall will start in his place.

Washington is like a new man this season, especially of late. He has played great ball and looks fully engaged over the past few games. It also appears as though he re-worked his shot in the offseason, to positive effect. Against Utah, Washington was 6-of-10 from downtown and led all players in scoring. If he can be a bit more consistent with this type of play, he will add another dimension to the team on both sides of the ball.

Gafford had a quirky game. His stat line doesn't blow you away and his single rebound is somewhat infuriating, however he only played 18 minutes. In that time, he was effective and did what the team needed him to do. His highlight was blocking a three-point attempt, picking up the rejected ball in stride and going in for a monster break-away slam to get the crowd into it.

If Clayton stays consistent, he is going to make Masai Ujiri, Mike Schmitz and Dusty May look like geniuses. On a team light on well-known guards, the Mavs front office picked up Clayton at the very start of the season and he's been great for his role. He shot only 1-of-5 from deep Friday night, but has been on fire from range to this part of the season and still managed to hit 50% of his eight shots overall. He's a capable player that can spell Irving and seeing him play well is no longer a surprise.

Johnson was an absolute beast. Compared to his first three games, Johnson looks like a different person. Whether it was shin splints early on or just acclimating to the NBA game, Johnson has experienced a major growth spurt. He is fouling less, playing better within the system and is much more explosive than he was early on. He was all over the boards, led the team in plus/minus with a +20 and was within the flow of the game throughout. This was a coming out party that was great to witness.

Dallas needed a win after getting smacked by the Kings and losing a close one to the Spurs. The Mavs couldn't sleep on the single-win Jazz (who are probably better than their record) like they did with the Kings. It appears they learned from that experience and played through some struggles to pull away. This also gives them their second Emirates Cup win in as many tries, while boosting their point differential as well.

I invite you to follow me @_80MPH on X, and check back often at Mavs Moneyball for all the latest on the Dallas Mavericks.

#2881. FINAL: Oklahoma State Cowboys 66, K-State Wildcats 28

Drew Mestemaker threw for 313 yards on 22-28 passing, including 4 touchdowns passing (plus one rushing and one receiving), and Caleb Hawkins had 175 yards rushing with three touchdowns and 71 yards receiving with a score too, racking up 246 yards of offense on a defenseless Wildcat defense as Oklahoma State rolled to a 66-28 win over Kansas State this afternoon at Bill Snyder Family Stadium in Manhattan, Kansas.

K-State (4-5, 1-5 Big 12) was wildly overmatched on the day. Nothing went right, and the Cats were already in a 28-7 hole after 15 minutes, a deficit they had no hope of erasing in the face of the Oklahoma State (6-3, 4-2 Big 12) offense.

Things appeared to start with a bang as Monterrio Elston returned the opening kickoff 72 yards, but a holding call erased it. But the offense went 3-and-out, and then things did start with a bang for the Cowboys, who got a 31-yard run from Caleb Hawkins and a 32-yard touchdown pass from Drew Mestemaker to Justin Bowick.

Staring at a second 3-and-out, Barnett found Salley for a 73-yard touchdown pass as Oklahoma State badly blew the coverage, leaving Salley open with nobody within ten yards of him. But the Wildcat defense was again helpless on the return drive, and Oklahoma State marched right back downfield, scoring on a 21-yard run by Hawkins.

The Cats managed to move the ball on the following drive, but facing 4th-and-1 at the Cowboy 36, Barnett was stopped short. Hawkins scored again on a 37-yard run a few plays later, and the Cowboys were up 21-7 with five minutes still left in the opening frame. Then Garrett Oakley didn't catch a pass he should have, the ball bouncing into the hands of Quinton Hammonds, and Oklahoma State had the ball on the 11-yard line. Three plays later, Mestemaker found Morgan McPhaul for a 3-yard score.

The Cats then couldn't move the chains, but Wyatt Young fumbled after catching a Mestemaker pass, and Rex Van Wyhe recovered. But the Cats went 3-and-out; however, after the punt Mestemaker was picked off by Logan Bartley for an 18-yard return score, and the Cats closed the gap to 28-14.

An onside kick attempt failed, giving Oklahoma State a short field. A pass interference call on a throw to the end zone and plus yardage on the next four plays left the Cats defending 1st-and-goal at the one. They did not; Mestemaker carried it in himself, and it was 35-14.

The Cats then went three-and-out, and Oklahoma State had to put after Bartley almost intercepted another pass, but then Barnett was intercepted by LaDanian Fields, Rodney's cousin and former teammate. Three plays later, Hawkins scored on a 9-yard run. The Cats had to punt again, and the Cowboys scored again, a 19-yard catch by Miles Coleman which staked Oklahoma State to a 49-14 halftime lead.

The Wildcat defense forced a 3-and-out after the break, and Dillon Duff started the second half at quarterback. He led the Cats downfield, striking Salley with an 18-yard touchdown pass, but the defense gave it right back on a 5-yard pass to Hawkins. Then LaDainian Fields picked off Duff for his second interception of the day, and three plays later Wyatt Young took a jet sweep then threw back to Mestemaker for a 5-yard score, giving the Cowboy quarterback passing, rushing, and receiving touchdowns in the same game and a 63-21 lead for Oklahoma State — the most points they have ever scored against the Wildcats.

With the game far out of reach, K-State did finally manage a drive — a 15-play march covering 66 yards which took half a quarter, blessed relief for everyone watching the game on both sides. Rodney Fields finally scored on a 1-yard run to make the score 63-28. Sam Keltner later added a 23-yard field goal to cap off the scoring.

As it happened, Duff was the more effective quarterback than Barnett today. Both threw 17 passes and had one touchdown; Duff completed 10 for 94 yards and a pick and had two yards on the ground, Barnett completed only 7 for 106 and two interceptions, adding 23 yards on 10 carries. Field had 87 yards and a score on 20 carries (plus one catch for 12 yards), and the entire rest of the team only managed 15 yards.

Salley caught 3 balls for 100 yards and two scores. Jaron Tibbs caught 6 for 42, and Linkon Cure had 5 for 33. Josh Manning and Oakley each had a short catch.

The Cowboys outgained K-State 565-327, 342-200 in the air, 22-3127 on the ground. There really aren't any ancillary stats that matter in the face of that; K-State simply got railed.

Really, that's it. This game was an absolute disaster, the defense is a disaster, the offense is a disaster, and we've all learned all of this over the last month. There's nothing new and interesting to say after this trainwreck that hasn't already been said.

There isn't even a decision here. Derrick Salley had 100 yards receiving, which was 30% of K-State's entire offense. Logan Bartley had a pick, almost another one, two pass breakups on top of that, and five tackles.

K-State visits TCU next week, game time and network to be determined.

#2879. Broncos sign Taylor Rapp to their practice squad and will elevate him and RB Cody Schrader for the game vs. the Panthers

I also wouldn't be surprised if reserve safety JL Skinner gets some added playing time after his big-time hit on Chiefs running back Kenneth Walker at the goal line during last Sunday's thrilling victory over the Kansas City Chiefs. Either way, the Broncos will need to rely on their safety depth for at least one more week.

As for Cody Schrader, he will serve as the Broncos RB3 for the second straight week. Look for another heavy dose of JK Dobbins and rookie Jonah Coleman at running back, with Schrader serving as depth and playing special teams. With both RJ Harvey and Tyler Badie on the injured reserve, Schrader is getting the call at running back. The Broncos have been calling around about running backs prior to the upcoming trade deadline, so this might be the final week we see Schrader elevated, but time will tell.

The team also announced that they have activated wide receiver Pat Bryant from the injured reserve while also placing defensive lineman Eyioma Uwazurike on the injured reserve. They also signed reserve cornerback/returner Sam Fresch Jr. from the practice squad to the active roster. So, it was an active Saturday for the Broncos when it came to roster transactions.

#2876. What Josh Heupel said about officials call on Tennessee's DaSaahn Brame in end zone

Tennessee football coach Josh Heupel got an unsatisfying explanation from officials on a key pass into the end zone during the Vols win over Kentucky at Neyland Stadium on Nov. 7.

With the Vols struggling to find consistency in the passing game, and nursing a four-point lead over the Wildcats, quarterback George MacIntyre fired a pass for tight end DaSaahn Brame into the far corner of the end zone. The pass was ruled incomplete, but replay showed that Brame had two hands on the ball and got at least one foot down.

Tennessee coach Josh Heupel said officials did review the play but didn't agree that Brame met the criteria for a touchdown.

"They said that they did review it," Heupel said postgame. "I asked them."

No. 16 Tennessee scored 21 unanswered points in the fourth quarter to secure a blowout, 34-6 victory over the No. 7 Wildcats and make the controversial call a moot point.

Brame suffered an injury on the play, hitting the back of his head on the turf. He was helped off the field and did not return to the game, finishing with one reception for three yards.

#2875. 2026 Week 9 rooting guide for Cowboys fans

After their latest disaster against the Cardinals, the Dallas Cowboys feel more like a charity case than a contender. But as fitting as a classic Jim Mora "Playoffs?!" meme feels at this point, Dallas is still mathematically alive in both the NFC East and the Wild Card picture. If they do somehow turn things around this season, what else needs to go right to make a postseason appearance possible?

Our Week 9 rooting guide is for those still hopped up on hopium. Because Dallas probably won't have its own first-round pick in 2027, so rooting for draft position just isn't as fun or interesting. We'll flip over to that once it's clearly the only path worth considering.

1. San Francisco 49ers 6-1 (4-1 vs NFC)2. Minnesota Vikings 6-1 (4-1)3. New York Giants 4-2-1 (2-2-1)4. Carolina Panthers 3-4 (3-3)5. Chicago Bears 6-2 (4-2)6. Detroit Lions 5-2 (4-1)7. Green Bay Packers 5-3 (4-3)_______________________________8. Seattle Seahawks 4-4 (1-3)9. Los Angeles Rams 4-4 (2-2)10. Tampa Bay Buccaneers 3-5 (3-2)11. Atlanta Falcons 3-5 (3-3)12. Arizona Cardinals 3-5 (2-4)11. Dallas Cowboys 3-5 (2-4)

Thanks mostly to head-to-head losses, the Cowboys are at the back of the pack of 3-5 teams. That's quickly rectified if they can start piling up some wins, but it does make the current wild card picture look quite daunting.

Thankfully, the climb in the NFC East isn't so steep. The Giants' lead isn't that great, especially as their schedule is about to stiffen up. It only takes a couple of weeks for division contention to feel very possible, and the mood around here to change.

Much of that hinges on this Sunday, of course. If the Cowboys can't even put down the Colts, who've already started selling off assets in a clear surrender of this season, then it will be time for them to consider a similar course. With the trade deadline on Tuesday, it will be a major decision point for the front office as to how it wants to treat this current season and the future of the franchise.

So, if Dallas can do its part in Indianapolis, what else can we root for from the Week 9 schedule?

While we don't want Philly or Washington suddenly becoming problems for us in the division, New York and Los Angeles are bigger fish to fry. Obviously, we want the G-Men to start taking losses and becoming easier to catch. We also don't need the Rams suddenly finding themselves and remaining a barrier to a potential wild card spot.

Plus, while Dallas probably won't be drafting ahead of the Eagles or Commanders in the 2027 first round, we certainly wouldn't mind seeing as far down the draft order as possible.

Both of these matchups feature a team that Dallas has lost to this season, but we're treating them differently. Arizona doesn't seem like a threat to contend later this year, while Seattle could easily remain a problem in the wild card race. So the long game favors a Cardinals upset here.

Tampa Bay, on the other hand, does have some staying power once Baker Mayfield returns. And with the Bears at 6-2, they're probably not going to fall into Dallas' range in the wild card. If anything, they're still the arguable favorite to win the NFC North.

What a week for the AFC to do us some favors. These are all NFC teams currently ahead of Dallas in the standings, so a wave of AFC wins would be most welcome.

Granted, even if Dallas gets its stuff together, we're probably not touching the Niners, Vikings, or a few other frontrunners come January. But when there are zero pains from the AFC teams' success, we'll take any gains we can get.

The Green Bay-New England game is of added interest because of 2027 draft implications. If Dallas ends up using the Packers' first-round pick next April, which is highly likely, we certainly want it as high in the order as possible. We'll gladly sacrifice the value of the seventh-round pick that the Patriots owe us, too.

This heavy interconference slate rounds out the week in terms of Dallas' rooting interests. The Steelers are on their bye, so we'll check back in with them and the fourth-rounder they owe us next week.

We'll also see what happens with our own goofy team this Sunday, and how that shapes our rooting guides going forward.

#2874. 4 Head Coach Candidates Ohio State Should Target If Ryan Day Leaves

It's not even Thanksgiving, and Ohio State's football season is over after it fell to Oregon at home 41-28 on Saturday, leaving the Buckeyes with a 6-3 record.

Of course, fans are blaming head coach Ryan Day, and some have even called for him to be fired. However, that seems a bit ridiculous.

Day is one of the best coaches in college football, and Ohio State shouldn't consider firing him, but if he leaves this offseason, he could take a position in the NFL.

If Day leaves Ohio State this winter, here are a few candidates the Buckeyes could consider to replace him.

The first call should be to Notre Dame head coach Marcus Freeman, as he has ties to Ohio State. Freeman played linebacker for the Buckeyes from 2004 to 2008 under head coach Jim Tressel. He has coached Notre Dame for five seasons and has a 50-13 record. He led the Fighting Irish to the 2024 national championship game, where he fell to Ohio State.

This past offseason, there was speculation that the New York Giants wanted to bring in Freeman as their next head coach, but he declined.

It would be hard to get Freeman to leave Notre Dame, but returning to where he played college football and the state he grew up in could bring him back to Columbus.

Ohio State's defensive coordinator Matt Patricia taking over the head coaching job wouldn't be that crazy if Day jumped ship for the NFL. If Ohio State can't land a top college football head coach, then maybe Patricia is the route the Buckeyes take.

Patricia coached the Detroit Lions from 2018 to 2020 and served as an NFL defensive coordinator with the New England Patriots from 2012 to 2017.

Candle is an Ohio native and attended Mount Union. He has coaching experience in Ohio, from his time as a WR coach and offensive coordinator at Mount Union to his head coaching role at Toledo from 2015 to 2025.

The 46-year-old head coach took over UConn this season and had a 4-4 record heading into Week 10.

Many Ohio State fans have been calling for an Ohio native to take over the head coaching job for a while, and Candle could be an option.

This is just a thought to consider if Meyer wants to return to coaching. While it's unlikely Meyer will coach again, this matters more if Ohio State struggles to find a head coach after Day leaves. The Buckeyes could potentially call Meyer to take over for a few seasons.

Meyer coached Ohio State from 2012 to 2018, finishing 83-9 and winning the 2014 championship.

It's pretty obvious that Ohio State has the right head coach in Day and shouldn't look to fire him because the options don't move the needle much.

#2867. Duke 41, N.C. State 3: Five Key Plays

Duke notched its seventh win of the season with a 41-3 dismantling of the N.C. State Wolfpack on the road in Raleigh. The Blue Devils are now 7-2 overall and 4-2 in the Atlantic Coast Conference.

Heisman Trophy candidate Nate Sheppard rushed for 217 yards and three touchdowns on 18 carries to pace Duke.

Quarterback Walker Eget completed 22 of 31 pass attempts for 208 yards and one touchdown. Jaivon Solomon recorded five receptions for 71 yards and a touchdown, and Jonah Burton had five receptions for 52 yards. Burton had multiple drive-extending receptions on third down.

Duke's defense completely shut down the N.C. State running game limiting the Wolfpack to 1.3 yards per carry (32 yards on 25 carries).

Play One: On Duke's first possession, the Blue Devils faced a third down and 10 yards to go. Quarterback Walker Eget found wide receiver Jonah Burton on the right side of the field for a 13-yards completion and first down. On the next play, running back Nate Sheppard took an inside hand-off and broke free for a 69-yard touchdown run and 7-0 Duke lead.

Play Two: Early in the 2nd quarter, Duke faced a fourth down and one yard to go at the N.C. State 29-yard line. Nate Sheppard took the direct snap out of the Wildcat Formation and gained 7 yards for a first down. Four plays later Sheppard once again took the direct snap and rushed 8 yards for his second touchdown and a 14-3 Duke lead.

Play Three: With 26 seconds remaining in the 1st half, on third down and four yards to go, Walker Eget completed a pass on the left sideline to wide receiver Javion Solomon for an 18-yard touchdown. Fifteen of the 18 yards were yards after catch.

Play Four: With one second remaining in the 1st half, N.C. State attempted a 50-yard field goal. The kick was short and fielded by safety Andrew Pellicciotta in the back of the end zone. Pellicciotta returned the missed kick 109 yards for a Duke touchdown and 31-3 halftime lead for the Blue Devils. Pellicciotta's run was terrific as he weaved around and through Wolfpack defenders.

Play Five: With three minutes and 52 seconds remaining in the 3rd quarter, Nate Sheppard took the direct snap and rushed 4-yards for his third touchdown of the game and a 38-3 Duke lead.

#2862. Bill Belichick praises Rob Gronkowski for 'well-deserved' induction into Patriots Hall of Fame: 'Proud of Rob and what he's accomplished'

Though now with UNC, Bill Belichick still has a lot of fondness for some of his former New England Patriots players. Following the Tar Heels' 38-21 win over UConn, Belichick was asked about former tight end Rob Gronkowski, who was inducted into the Patriots Hall of Fame on Saturday.

The normally stone-faced coach was surprisingly warm while speaking about Gronkowski. Belichick said that the tight end's induction was "well-deserved" and praised Gronkowski as one of the best in his position.

"[He] was the best tight end in football during his time and one of the best of all time," Belichick said. "[I'm] proud of Rob and what he's accomplished. He came a long, long way from barely playing in college in Arizona. … Credit to him, his work ethic, his toughness and his dependability. I mean, it's a hard position to play but he excelled at it for a long time.

"Wouldn't have won as many games as I did without him, I can tell you that. He's a big part of it," Belichick added.

Gronkowski was drafted by the Patriots in the second round of the 2010 NFL Draft out of Arizona, spending nine seasons in New England during Belichick's 23-year stint as the team's head coach. The tight end won three Super Bowls alongside Belichick and longtime quarterback Tom Brady, with the Patriots going a combined 113-31 during Gronkowski's time there.

Gronkowski, who was elected to the Hall of Fame in his first year of eligibility, was honored by the team Saturday afternoon. Brady was among the other Hall of Famers present at the induction ceremony.

In a speech that included stories about Belichick, Gronkowski ended the ceremony in true Gronk fashion: "I am a Patriot for life. Now let's fiesta!"

Belichick, meanwhile, celebrated with a much-needed win that brings UNC to a 5-4 overall record.

#2861. What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique

What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

They're a team of superstars that don't play superstar basketball.

That's the beauty of this 76ers team. They play a brand of unselfish team-first basketball you rarely see from a team loaded with stars.

LeBron James is one of the two or three greatest players in NBA history, a four-time MVP, a four-time NBA champion, a 1st-ballot Hall of Famer.

Joel Embiid is a seven-time all-star, a former MVP, an Olympic gold medalist and a likely Hall of Famer.

Jaylen Brown is a five-time all-star, an NBA champion, a career 20 points-per-game scorer.

Tyrese Maxey is a two-time all-star, one of only two NBA guards averaging 27.0 points per game over the last four years.

Four certified megastars on one team. And somehow they've figured out how to leave their egos at the front door and play smart, tough, disciplined team basketball, the style of ball you just never see on these superstar-laden teams.

On offense, they patiently work the ball around, always looking for the open man or a positive matchup. You've got this bunch of big-name, high-priced All-Stars who are just as happy to pass the ball as shoot it. That never happens.

The 76ers are sixth in the NBA in assists, and they're one of only four teams with twice as many assists as turnovers. Smart, unselfish, patient, disciplined.

When an all-timer like LeBron averages 7.3 assists per game that speaks volumes. LeBron is fifth in the NBA in assists per game among forwards, which is wild. You can see the joy on his face when one of his passes leads to a basket. When one of the greatest players in history has that mentality, it rubs off on everyone around him.

Embiid hasn't really got going yet on offense and still takes too many 3's, but he's also embraced the team-first mentality, and his 4.8 assists per game are more than one full assist above his career average and 4th-most in the NBA among centers or forward-centers. And still one of the most dominating defensive players in the league.

Brown is averaging 27.0 points, which you expect, but he's also averaging 4.3 assists per game, significantly above his career average of 3.8 coming into the season. He's fit in so well so fast you forget its his first season here.

And what can you even say about Maxey? Just a beast. He's averaging nearly 30 points per game but never wastes a possession, always makes the right decision with the ball in his hands and is still finding time to average 4.3 assists per game.

With James, Embiid, Brown and Maxey, the 76ers are the only NBA team with four guys averaging at least 16 points and 4 assists per game. Only the Pacers, Magic and Raptors have three.

But where we're really seeing this group playing out of their minds is on the defensive end, where this bunch of all-stars, including two newcomers, has been astounding.

The 76ers have the 2nd-best defensive efficiency rating in the league, behind only the Pistons, who they play Wednesday night, and they're allowing the 2nd-fewest points in the league, also behind Detroit. Last year, they allowed 123 points per game through eight games. This year they're at 111.

They're holding teams to 43 percent shooting from the field, 3rd-best in the league and their lowest since 2011-12. Their 9.3 steals per game are 6th-most.

All five starters are among the top 25 players in the league in defensive rating – James is fourth, Embiid 11th, Edgecombe 15th, Maxey 22nd and Brown 23rd. How is that even possible?

It's rare to see superstar-caliber guys play as hard on the defensive end as the offensive end. Everybody on this team embraces it. They hustle, they work, they communicate and most importantly they commit to it.

The biggest concern when the 76ers built this dream team was that it would be a team of individuals out for themselves, but the reality has been the opposite.

In a very short period of time, they've learned how to play together. How to play for each other.

And we haven't even mentioned V.J. Edgecomb, who at 21 years old has figured out how to assimilate into this loaded lineup, never force anything, wait for his opportunities and make huge shots when he gets his turn. Edgecombe is at 12.7 points and 3.0 assists per game, and his numbers are understandably down a bit from last year, but if that bothers him it sure doesn't show.

And the role players – Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Jabari Walker – have each adopted the same ego-free style of basketball, supporting the starters however they need to.

Nick Nurse deserves a ton of credit for getting this bunch to play this brand of hoops. It's not easy to get any NBA player to put team goals ahead of individual goals. But he's got Hall of Famers and all-stars doing it and having a blast along the way. They all seem to love playing basketball the right way and they seem to love playing together.

The 76ers are 7-1 and looking like a real threat to win the East, and they're doing it by playing the game the right way.

By committing to each other and not themselves.

#2859. Bainbridge's Dodge claims Class 2A boys cross country championship

Last year, Bainbridge cross country standout Ryan Dodge helped the Spartans win the Class 2A team championship at Sun Willows Golf Course in Pasco.

As a senior, Dodge felt what it was like to reach the top of the podium as an individual.

Dodge crossed the finish line in 15 minutes, 27.8 second to easily win the 2A boys race Saturday, Nov. 7. Dodge led the field at the 1-mile and 2-mile splits and wasn't challenged down the stretch. North Central senior Elliot Yegge took second in 15:44, while Kingston senior Timothy Clark-Johnston finished fourth in 15:47.4.

Bainbridge took seventh as a team, one spot ahead of Kingston. Olympic finished 12th.

In what proved to be a much faster 3A boys race, 2025 champion Mana Voss of Central Kitsap placed fifth in 15:25.2. Hermiston (Ore.) junior Leroy Lozano Mejia placed first in 14:45.9 and Shorewood senior Elijah Graves placed second in 14:55.9. Gig Harbor High was the boys team state champion.

In the 2A girls competition, Kingston senior Emma Grice ran 19:05.5 to place eighth, two spots ahead of Bainbridge senior Evelyn Shutske (19:10.1). Bainbridge junior Vega Hendrickson took 15th and Olympic senior Brynn Fulton placed 19th. As a team, Bainbridge finished sixth.

In the 1B boys race, Crosspoint Christian junior Haowu Liu placed 19th.

#2855. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2853. 1 thing I loved, 2 things I hated: Michigan Football vs MSU

The Michigan Wolverines lost to Michigan State for the first time since 2021, this time by a final score of 15-6. The Spartans largely owned the game from start to finish, as the final score is not as close as it may appear.

Here is one thing I loved and two things I hated from tonight's game.

It took the Wolverines a little while to get things going offensively, as the first possession resulted in a Tommy Carr interception while the second possession was a three-and-out.

But things clicked on Michigan's third drive of the game, and it was thanks to the power run game. Running back Bryson Kuzdzal took a handoff five yards up the middle on the first play of the drive. After a pass by Carr to Kuzdzal for a first down, Kuzdzal picked up 16 yards up the middle to pick up another first down. Kudzdal then a nine-yard run to pick up another first down for the Wolverines.

It's a shame the drive ended in a missed field goal, but the Wolverines picked up 71 yards on the drive, with 49 of them being on the ground. It was a solid drive that should have resulted in points, but it was a small glimmer of light in an otherwise supermassive black hole.

But other that that drive, I can't say anything else positively about Michigan's offense. It was flat, uninspired and unsuccessful for a majority of the game. The Wolverines compiled a total of 64 yards on their other eight drives. All in all, Michigan threw for 138 yards and ran for 54 yards; Carr threw two interceptions and Kuzdzal lost a fumble. They only scored when the game was already over, and then proceeded to miss the two-point try.

The offense should feel ashamed of this performance. To be shut out against one of the worst defenses in the country at home is absolutely embarrassing.

We've been very complimentary of Jay Hill and the defensive unit this season, but cracks in the armor started to show last weekend against Rutgers. Things weren't all that great against the Spartans on Saturday night either.

Michigan's defense was on the field a lot on Saturday. The defense gave up drives consisting of six (three times), nine, 10 (twice) and 17 plays. The Spartans owned time of possession, taking up 38:07 of game clock. They wore out the Michigan defense early and often, running 75 total plays.

MSU wide receiver Chrishon McCray led the way with six receptions for 92 yards. Bryson Williams also had one explosive reception for 36 yards, while Brandon Tullis led them on the ground with 25 carries for 86 yards.

The offense certainly didn't do the defense any favors tonight, but to give up nearly 300 total yards of offense to that team is unacceptable.

In a game where there's not much to love, I would like to just give a quick shoutout to edge rusher John Henry Daley, who was on another level early on Saturday night.

His first sack happened because he pushed the MSU left tackle into quarterback Alessio Milivojevic. This was one of the highlights of the game for the Wolverines.

Daley had another sack later in the evening. He totaled two sacks, 2.5 tackles for loss, eight total tackles and one forced fumble in this game.

What did you love and hate from the MSU game? Let me know down in the comments below!

#2851. Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch

Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers and Cavaliers have already played one game that looked like two.

Cleveland led by 29 points during the Sixers' home opener on Oct. 22 and carried a 69-45 advantage into halftime. Philadelphia responded by winning the second half, 69-37, and completing a 114-106 comeback.

It was the Sixers' largest comeback since the NBA began maintaining detailed play-by-play data in 1997-98. Only nine other teams during that span had overcome a deficit of at least 29 points.

Fifteen days later, the teams will meet again Friday night at Rocket Arena. This time, the matchup counts toward the NBA Cup standings.

The 8-1 Sixers have won five consecutive games and opened group play with a 98-93 victory over the Knicks. The 4-4 Cavs have dropped two straight and will make their Cup debut.

Here are five numbers that help frame the rematch.

Philadelphia's comeback required more than a hot shooting stretch.

The Sixers committed 15 turnovers in the first half, allowing Cleveland to build a 22-6 advantage in points off turnovers. Their pressure then changed the game after halftime.

The Cavs committed 21 turnovers by the end of the night. What had been a 16-point Cleveland advantage in points off turnovers finished even at 22 apiece.

"They upped their pressure and physicality," Cavs head coach Kenny Atkinson said after the game. "It was pretty clear. We did not handle it well."

The Sixers scored 64 of their 69 second-half points through their starting lineup. Tyrese Maxey finished with 28 points, and Philadelphia turned a game that appeared finished into a franchise-record comeback.

The first half of the opening meeting now looks like an exception.

The Sixers have committed 12.9 turnovers per 100 possessions this season, the NBA's second-lowest rate. They are also third in turnover differential, averaging 3.4 fewer giveaways than their opponents.

That is notable for a team that entered the season intending to play faster. Philadelphia has pushed the ball without routinely surrendering possessions.

LeBron James' presence as the primary facilitator has helped. So has Maxey's willingness to move the ball when defenses trap him. Joel Embiid has averaged 5.2 assists, third among qualified centers, and the Sixers have increasingly trusted VJ Edgecombe to make decisions late in games.

Cleveland's defense has not forced many mistakes. The Cavs enter Friday near the bottom of the league in opponent turnover rate.

The first meeting swung when the Sixers stopped giving away possessions and forced Cleveland into mistakes of its own.

The Sixers have allowed 105.1 points per 100 possessions, the NBA's second-best defensive rating. Cleveland enters with the league's ninth-ranked offense.

The Cavs are the only top-10 offense the Sixers have faced through their first nine games.

Philadelphia has held opponents to a 50 percent effective field-goal percentage and 31.8 percent shooting from three-point range. Its defensive shot profile has been encouraging, too. Only 46 percent of opposing three-point attempts have been classified as wide open, the fifth-lowest share in the league, and opponents have taken just 22 percent of their threes from the corners.

There is reason for caution. Based on the quality of shots Philadelphia has allowed, only Washington's opponents have underperformed their expected effective field-goal percentage by a wider margin. The Sixers have defended well, but opponents are unlikely to remain this cold indefinitely.

Cleveland, with Donovan Mitchell, James Harden and Evan Mobley, can test whether those numbers hold against high-level individual shot creation.

The Cavs have attempted exactly 42 three-pointers per game and made 37.2 percent of them.

Cleveland went 7 for 29 from beyond the arc in Philadelphia. Mitchell, Harden and De'Andre Hunter combined to shoot 3 for 17. The Cavs scored 60 points in the paint and still reached 106, but their 24.1 percent performance from three prevented them from stopping the Sixers' comeback.

Friday's game could look considerably different if Cleveland generates its normal volume.

The Sixers have controlled the three-point line well so far, but Mitchell is coming off a 27-point performance against Oklahoma City and scored 32 in the first meeting with Philadelphia. Harden added 16 points that night, although his six turnovers became costly as the Sixers increased their pressure.

Philadelphia has been outrebounded by 5.1 per game. The Cavs rank sixth with 47.5 rebounds per contest.

The Sixers have already shown they can survive a major deficit on the glass. They beat Milwaukee by 11 despite getting outrebounded by 22. That is not a formula they will want to test regularly against Cleveland's size.

Mobley had 18 points and eight rebounds in the first meeting. Jarrett Allen added 14 points and seven boards, helping Cleveland win the rebounding battle, 46-44.

Whether Allen plays Friday remains uncertain.

Cleveland listed Allen as questionable because of a right knee injury after he left Wednesday's loss to Oklahoma City in the first quarter. Allen collided knee-to-knee with Thunder forward Chet Holmgren and did not return.

The Cavs also listed Peyton Watson as questionable with right hamstring soreness and Tyrese Proctor as questionable with an ankle injury. Proctor left Wednesday's game during the fourth quarter. Jaylon Tyson is out with a right ankle sprain.

Allen's availability could significantly alter the matchup on the glass. If he sits, Thomas Bryant would likely handle more of the center minutes behind Mobley. If he plays, Cleveland will have the same starting frontcourt that outrebounded the Sixers during the first meeting.

Atkinson has emphasized the possession battle during Cleveland's two-game slide.

"You've got to get more shots than the other team," he said this week. "Rebounding is a big part of it."

That also carries added importance in the NBA Cup, where point differential is the second tiebreaker after head-to-head results.

Philadelphia enters Friday at 1-0 with a plus-five differential in East Group B. Miami is also 1-0 at plus-20, while Cleveland is playing its first group game.

The Sixers already know they can erase 29 points against the Cavs. A cleaner start would be far more useful than proving it twice.

#2849. Beede’s Breakdown: Shorthanded Magic fight but fall in overtime at Warriors

It was always going to be an uphill battle for the Magic without Paolo Banchero (right high ankle sprain) and Jalen Suggs (right knee bone bruise) against the Warriors on Saturday night.

Then in the hours leading up to the game in San Francisco, Magic guard Desmond Bane was ruled out because of an illness.

Missing three starters, Orlando didn't back down, fought hard throughout the night and forced overtime inside Chase Center. But the Magic fell short in the extra five-minute period on the road in a game that featured 10 lead changes.

Behind 41 points from 11-time All-NBA guard Stephen Curry, the Warriors escaped on top, 126-119, to earn their seventh straight victory and hand Orlando its fourth straight loss.

Trailing the Warriors 110-108 with less than 30 seconds remaining in regulation, Magic forward Jamal Cain (18 points) tipped in an offensive rebound that sent the game to overtime tied at 110.

Tristan da Silva's fifth 3-pointer of the night gave Orlando a 118-117 lead with 2:24 left in overtime. Curry then converted an and-one opportunity to put Golden State back in front for good, 120-118, at the 1:38 mark of the extra period.

Despite a combined 86 points from Franz Wagner (30), da Silva (19), Wendell Carter Jr. (19) and Anthony Black (18), the Warriors (7-3) outscored the Magic 16-9 in overtime.

The Magic (2-7) continue their four-game road trip on the West Coast at Sacramento on Tuesday night. Orlando travels to Utah the following night.

Da Silva made his first start of the season when he opened alongside Jase Richardson, Black, Wagner and Carter at Golden State.

The Warriors scored the first six points of the game and eventually took a 23-9 lead with four minutes remaining in the first quarter.

Orlando went more than five minutes from the 8:01 mark to the 2:42 mark of the opening quarter without scoring. Golden State took a 31-19 lead at the end of the 12-minute period when only three members of the Magic scored (Wagner, da Silva and Cain).

Richardson never found his footing on offense when he missed all six of his 3-point attempts and shot 1-for-13 from the floor for just two points in 30 minutes.

Trailing by 16 points at the half, the Magic punched back in a major way in the third quarter.

Layups from da Silva and Goga Bitadze got Orlando within two points of the Warriors, 69-67, with 4:33 left in the third quarter. A pull-up jumper by Jase Richardson tied the game at 69 and a 3-pointer from da Silva gave Orlando its first lead at 72-69 with 3:23 remaining in the frame.

The Magic went on a 20-5 run that stretched from the 6:08 mark of the third down to the 49-second mark of the quarter. Black scored 11 of his first 13 points in the frame while da Silva added 10 of his first 13.

After not leading once in the first half, Orlando held a 78-77 lead entering the fourth quarter.

Cain scored eight in the first quarter by sinking two late 3-pointers and adding a two-handed slam dunk with less than a minute left in the opening frame.

The Magic forward was active on the glass with 12 rebounds and he added one steal and one block on defense. The performance marked the first double-double of Cain's NBA career.

In addition, veteran guard Jevon Carter (nine points) hit back-to-back 3-pointers that powered an 8-0 second-quarter run. He later hit a third 3-pointer in the same frame.

Still, Orlando's bench was outscored 42-31 by Golden State's reserves, who were led by 11 points from Georges Niang (3-for-3 from distance).

Although the Magic weren't accurate from 3-point range on a night they shot 18-for-54 on the perimeter (33%), they did a solid job crashing the offensive glass.

For the night, Orlando held a 24-3 advantage in offensive rebounding, which led to extra scoring chances.

The Magic outscored Golden State 33-2 in second-chance scoring and shot 12-for-22 in the second-chance opportunities. Wendell Carter Jr.'s offensive rebound and putback layup tied the game at 98 with 3:54 left in the fourth quarter.

Jason Beede can be reached at jbeede@orlandosentinel.com

#2847. Roquan Smith hit with 2 NFL fines for his play in Ravens vs. Bills

Ravens linebacker Roquan Smith was hit with two fines for his play in Week 8's loss to the Buffalo Bills. In total, Smith will pony up almost $36,000 for two penalties last Sunday.

One fine was connected to unnecessary roughness for this play, where Smith not only grabbed Bills quarterback Josh Allen's facemask, but twisted it almost 180 degrees.

The second fine stemmed from unnecessary roughness penalty for a hit on a defenseless player not even a full minute into the game.

Week 8's fines make three total for Smith during the 2026 season. He was also fined $11,941 for unnecessary roughness stemming from a face mask in Week 2. That's why Week 8's fines were almost $18,000 each — because they weren't the first this season.

No other Ravens players were fined in Week 8. No Bills players were fined for that contest.

The NFL donates half the fines it collects to the NFLPA's efforts to support retired players, and the other half to the NFL Foundation and organizations supporting youth football programs, community health and wellness, and athlete safety.

#2844. Stephen Curry scores 41 points as Warriors win seventh straight, beating Magic 126-119 in overtime

SAN FRANCISCO (AP) — Stephen Curry converted a three-point play with 1:38 left in overtime that put Golden State ahead then scored a layup in the final minute to finish with 41 points, six rebounds and six assists, and the Warriors held off the Orlando Magic 126-119 on Saturday night for their seventh straight win.

Wendell Carter Jr. missed two free throws with 1:15 remaining that would have tied the game before Curry came through again.

Warriors rookie Yaxel Lendeborg made a key 3-pointer in overtime and contributed 23 points and eight rebounds. A day earlier he shared a video of gifting his mother a home in California.

Golden State started 0-3 and now has its longest winning streak since seven in a row from March 3-15, 2025.

Franz Wagner had 30 points and 11 assists and Tristan da Silva and Carter each scored 19 for the Magic, who have lost four straight on the Warriors' home floor.

Brandin Podziemski scored 15 points and Draymond Green had a season-high 13 assists for Golden State.

The final minutes of regulation were a back-and-forth scoring frenzy. Curry made a go-ahead layup with 25.2 seconds left and Jamal Cain's putback with 2 seconds left on Orlando's third try in the final moments sent it into overtime. Cain had 18 points and 12 rebounds off the bench to help rally the Magic, who trailed 57-41 at halftime then led 78-77 going into the final period.

Curry recorded his second 40-point game this season and fourth in the last five with at least 30 points. It was his 16th 40-point effort after age 35, most in NBA history. LeBron James has 15 and Michael Jordan did it 12 times.

The 38-year-old Curry, in his 18th season, also became the seventh player ever with 26,000 points, 5,000 rebounds and 6,000 assists, according to ELIAS.

Georges Niang and Carter received double technicals at the 9:17 mark of the second quarter when they tussled after Niang grabbed the Orlando center in the paint. Security guards kept Green from getting involved following his ejection against Phoenix last Monday.

Niang notched his third straight double-digit scoring game with 11 points.

Warriors: Host Charlotte on Monday night.

#2842. Ultimate Fan: It’s a good time to open up the offense, keep the defensive pressure coming

Ultimate Fans, life could not get better in Broncos Country right now. Denver is coming off an incredible victory over KC, currently holding court at the top of the AFC West, before a short trip out East to play the Panthers in spectacular weather just in front of a much-needed bye week.

AND on top of that, we have a fabulous UFG from another younger fan and newbie to the series who knocked it out the park just like his predecessors.

While Ky Broncos Fan wasn't even alive during the John Elway Era, his momma raised him right and turned him on to the GOAT and early Broncos' Lombardis. Watching the Tebow-to-DT thriller turned him into a fan for life and his Broncos' Mount Rushmore is testament to his appreciation for Denver's incredible history. So that gives you a hint of the treat you are in for!

I'm going to be at this game, and my track record is pretty good, so let's assume it's going to continue!

MHR – First, we have to talk about that KC game. What in the world happened? How excited/nervous were you for those final 15 seconds?Ky Broncos Fan: What happened was some Mile High Magic! I was standing in front of the TV pacing like a coach, and when that obvious false start was called (no matter what Brittany Mahomes thinks), I knew fate was on our side. The defining play of that sequence in my mind was JL Skinner stuffing Walker at the line of scrimmage after being forced into the game due to injuries; that kind of grit and determination is emblematic of the whole team. Last Sunday was my birthday, and the KC game was the perfect gift!

MHR – We used to do a post-game story at Mile High Report asking, "what did you learn about the Broncos this week?" It's a great question, so have YOU learned about the Broncos the last few weeks?Ky Broncos Fan: I've learned that the Broncos are still elite in late game situations. Over and over against some of the best teams in the league (besides the drubbing of the Cardinals), they've proved they still know how to win with their backs against the wall. The Arizona win was a nice reminder that fans can expect the Broncos to beat the teams they're supposed to beat instead of every week feeling like a coin flip.

MHR – The Broncos sit atop the AFC West at 6-2, but they rank 30th in total offense. Bo Nix has looked much better in the wins over Arizona and Kansas City. Has he turned a corner, or is this offense still living on late comebacks? As I've asked many before, is a constant comeback-style game, Ky Broncos Fan: The offense is improving, but the late comebacks are still hiding deeper issues. Establishing the run early will go a long way for more offensive consistency, and luckily for Broncos fans, Jonah Coleman seems more than ready to take that on. As for Bo, we all fell in love with his fire and gunslinging mentality last season when it mattered most. As he continues to regain confidence following the ankle injury, Denver needs him to trust his eyes and take the shots when they're there. His aversion to negative plays is one of his best traits, but Denver is at its best when he's balancing that with more risk-taking.

MHR – Carolina has been without starting corners Jaycee Horn and Mike Jackson, and Akayleb Evans is also on injured reserve. How aggressive should Sean Payton/Davis Webb be with telling Bo Nix to throw downfield on Sunday? Courtland Sutton and Jaylen Waddle have been key to moving the chains but not super explosive…any chance we get a big day from either/both?Ky Broncos Fan: This game is the perfect opportunity to unleash the passing game. Utilize the running backs to get ahead of the chains, and then open up the playbook and test their secondary. Rushing success will open up some play-action opportunities, and overall, I think pushing the ball downfield in this game is necessary to gaining confidence and setting the passing game up for more success later in the season. Waddle's speed and elusiveness are nightmares for opposing defenders, and I think he's overdue for turning a quick slant up field for a long touchdown. I also think we're overdue for a Sutton long bomb TD. When Bo puts it in the right place, Sutton turns 50/50 balls into 80/20 balls, and I want to see him get some chances to convert big plays and gain confidence this week.

MHR – On the other hand, the Panthers' run defense is atrocious (ranked 30th)…is this a game for Jonah Coleman to really break out? After that 51-yard catch-and-run + 5-yard TD last week…maybe he's poised for a big second half finish to the season? How would you suggest using him and JK Dobbins this week?Ky Broncos Fan: I'm starting Jonah Coleman in fantasy again this week. I spent a decent portion of my free agency budget to get him, and the Panthers' defense is the perfect chance for him to show the front office we don't need to spend draft capital on Jonathan Taylor. Dobbins can still be a very effective runner when healthy, and the priority has to be keeping Dobbins healthy for a playoff run. I'd make Coleman the true bellcow and spell him with Dobbins occasionally.

MHR – Nix dismissed the "trap game" talk. But it's a 1 p.m EST kickoff (11 a.m. Mile High time) right after an emotional comeback. Are you concerned the offense comes out flat? How does Nix avoid the offense getting in its own way?Ky Broncos Fan: I'm concerned. As someone who lives in eastern time, the Broncos always seem to start flat when they come to my time zone. Nix can avoid the offense getting in its own way with one word: handoff. Let Coleman and Dobbins do their jobs, and use their success to test the secondary in favorable situations. There are times when the playcalling should favor some early down aggressiveness to put Carolina on their heels too, but those playcalls have to be high-completion percentage opportunities that Payton trusts the offense to execute.

MHR – The Broncos defense has been mostly outstanding, coming up big when it needs to, even if it gives up some plays. How should Vance Joseph plan for Bryce Young, who has been having a decent season getting the ball in the hands of reigning Offensive Rookie of the Year Tetairoa McMillan and Jalen Coker, an equally dangerous receiver?Ky Broncos Fan: The secondary can trust the front line to get home. Young is easily overwhelmed by pressure and possibly dealing with an undisclosed knee injury that is altering his mobility. Joseph doesn't need to blitz more; just changing up coverage looks enough to keep Young guessing will buy the front line the time it needs to make game-changing plays. McMillan and Coker are great young receivers, but Denver's secondary has stymied greater, more experienced players already this season.

MHR – How should Vance Joseph take advantage of a depleted offensive line standing in front of Bryce Young…does the QB even have a chance with Zach Allen, Nik Bonitto, Jonathan Cooper, Malcolm Roach coming at him? With 23 sacks as a team this season, how worried should Young be on Sunday?Ky Broncos Fan: Young should be terrified. Carolina's path to success is a quick short passing game to get the ball out and mitigate the pass rush, but as long as the secondary and linebackers stay disciplined, they should face 3rd and longs routinely that let Allen, Bonitto and the others feast. Young has issues with the ball getting batted down at the line of scrimmage too, and the defense should be able to exploit him and create some chaos. They've made better quarterbacks than Young look worse this year, and I'm excited to see what they can pull off tomorrow.

MHR – Alex Singleton was named AFC Defensive Player of the Month. He's not always the best in coverage, but he does fly to the ball. What kind of impact do you think he has on the defense?Ky Broncos Fan: He's the emotional heart of the defense. His passion and elite tackling fuel everyone else's fires, and while he's not the most agile speedster of a linebacker we've seen, he mostly keeps himself out of bad situations by reading plays well. Carolina should try to isolate him in coverage, but that's easier said than done.

MHR – Denver ranks 25th against the run, allowing 127.9 yards per game. Carolina is coming off a 10-day "mini-bye." Is the run defense a weakness the Panthers can exploit?Ky Broncos Fan: It unfortunately is. Carolina's running backs aren't world-beaters, but a lack of disciplined tackling will allow their run game to take off quickly. I'm no defensive guru, but I'm worried about playoff matchups with better running backs come January if Denver can't clean up their run defense by then.

MHR – We are halfway through the season and just before a bye. Who are your midseason MVPs – offense, defense, ST?Ky Broncos Fan: Offense: Garret Bolles. PFF has him as 3rd best tackle in football this season, and he hasn't given up a sack yet. His growth over his career is amazing, and Denver's lucky to have him. I hope he retires here. Defense: Alex Singleton. Leading the league in tackles is amazing, but time and time again, we see him make big plays when it matters most. ST: Will Lutz. He's a fantastic kicker who seems more accurate in bigger moments. Lutz is exactly the kind of kicker you want on your roster for a postseason run.

Stats for each Broncos RB? Coleman: 20 carries, 87 yards, 2 TD. Dobbins: 6 carries, 15 yards.

Number of yards receiving/TDs for each WR/TE? Sutton: 5 catches, 68 yards, 1 TD. Waddle: 6 catches, 82 yards, 1 TD. Mims: 2 catches, 46 yards. Franklin: 1 catch, 8 yards. Engram: 2 catches, 12 yards. Trautman: 1 catch, 18 yards.

Number of times Nix barely misses getting sacked? 4

Broncos player with the most tackles? Singleton

Who gets the first sack of the game for the Broncos? Allen

Who gets any sacks? Allen, Bonitto, Cooper, Singleton

How many INTs/FF/FR for the Panthers' D? 0 INTs, 1 FF, 1 FR.

How many INTs/FF/FR for the Broncos' D? 1 INT, 2 FF, 1 FR.

How many completely wrong DPI penalties called Sunday? 2 on Moss, 0 on others.

Favorite John Elway win outside of the Super Bowls? The Drive, I can't rewatch it without getting chills.

Favorite Broncos win ever? Super Bowl 50. This one's for Pat!

Favorite Peyton Manning win? His 7-TD decimation of the Ravens to open 2013.

Team you love to beat the most in the AFC West? The Chiefs. When the Raiders are relevant again it will be them.

Team you hate to lose to the most in the rest of the AFC? The Patriots.

NFC team you could cheer for in the Super Bowl? The Giants. John Harbaugh is a first class coach and human being. My Baltimore-native wife wishes they hadn't run him off to New York!

NFC team you want to face more than any other in the Super Bowl? The 49ers. A rematch with them and beating a Shanahan for Denver's 4th championship would be poetic.

Favorite game looking forward to this season? The Bills on Christmas Day.

Easiest game left on the schedule? Week 18 against the Chargers.

Toughest game left on the schedule? Week 17 at New England, but thank goodness the Bills are in Denver.

Favorite Broncos player on the current roster? Pat Surtain II.

Favorite Broncos player of all time not named John Elway? Demaryius Thomas.

Favorite new guy/rookie on the team? Jonah Coleman.

Food/Snack you have to eat on game day? Popcorn.

Favorite game analyst/commentator? Mike Tirico is always excellent.

If YOU were choosing your Broncos' Mt. Rushmore, who would be included? Floyd Little, John Elway, Peyton Manning, Randy Gradishar.

How did you become a Broncos fan? I didn't grow up particularly close to any NFL teams, but my mom had liked the Broncos since the 90s due to Elway. The first season I remember paying close attention to was the Tebow year (I was born in 2000). I vividly remember watching the entire Broncos-Steelers playoff game, and as I watched Demaryius Thomas fly down the sideline as Mile High erupted, I fell in love with the Broncos and have watched every game religiously ever since.

It was Belichick's first game back coaching in New England since his tenure with the NFL's Patriots came to an end.

"It's good to be back in New England," Belichick said. "It was an interesting game. I think UConn is a really challenging team, they put a lot of pressure on us.

"The team rebounded pretty well after a couple of tough losses."

Timir Hickman-Collins returned an interception 62 yards for a touchdown and had a fumble recovery to set up a 6-yard touchdown pass from Edwards to Jordan Shipp. Edwards had a fourth-down completion and put the game away with a 3-yard scoring pass to Jelani Thurman with 3:38 remaining after Jalon Thompson's interception gave the ball back to the Tar Heels.

"We've been really harping on (getting) turnovers, every day in practice we have been doing that," Hickman-Collins said. "Today, me and our DB (Jalon Thompson), a lot of us had turnovers."

Peyton Seelmann led North Carolina (5-4) with nine tackles and two sacks. Hayden Pegg had 12 tackles and an interception for UConn (4-5).

Belichick coached the Patriots to six Super Bowl titles. He played at Wesleyan University from 1971-74, which is about 20 miles away from Rentschler Field. Belichick nearly guided North Carolina to a win over No. 1 Miami last week.

Bob Diaco, North Carolina's defensive line coach, coached UConn from 2014-16. UConn won six games in Diaco's second season before losing to Marshall in the St. Petersburg Bowl. He was fired after a 3-9 record in 2016. Diaco went 11-26 in his three seasons with the Huskies.

North Carolina hosts Louisville on Nov. 14.

UConn is home against James Madison on Nov. 14.

#2839. Quentin Lake apologized to Justin Herbert for "deli meat" and "seeing ghosts" comments

Rams safety Quentin Lake got plenty of attention for comments he made about intercepting a pass thrown by Chargers quarterback Justin Herbert, but on reflection, Lake didn’t like the attention he generated.

Lake told the Los Angeles Times that he reached out to Herbert to apologize.

On a podcast hosted by Lions wide receiver Amon-Ra St. Brown, Lake laughed about how confident the Rams were heading into the game with the Chargers, seeing how much Herbert has been struggling this season.

“He was throwing deli meat out there,” Lake said on the podcast. “He’s seeing ghosts. I don’t know what’s going on, but we knew we were gonna get a couple. At least one.”

Lake and St. Brown were high school teammates, and Lake said that in the moment, he just felt like he was joking around with an old friend. When he saw how far his comments spread, however, Lake felt bad that he came across as disrespecting an opponent. Lake said he texted Herbert but hasn’t heard back from him.

“I don’t know if he read it,” Lake said. “He probably says, ‘I don’t care about it anymore. I’m not trying to see this. It doesn’t matter. You said what you said.’ But at the same time I . . . felt like it was right for me to at least say,’ Hey, man, I didn’t mean it in any disrespectful way.’”

Lake said he considers Herbert “a great quarterback” despite his struggles this season.

#2837. What Happened to Bryce Underwood? Why Michigan Is Playing QB Tommy Carr

ANN ARBOR, MICHIGAN - OCTOBER 24: Tommy Carr #14 of the Michigan Wolverines with Bryce Underwood #19 after a 13-6 overtime loss at Michigan Stadium on October 24, 2026 in Ann Arbor, Michigan. (Photo by Gregory Shamus/Getty Images) Getty

ANN ARBOR, MICHIGAN - OCTOBER 24: Tommy Carr #14 of the Michigan Wolverines with Bryce Underwood #19 after a 13-6 overtime loss at Michigan Stadium on October 24, 2026 in Ann Arbor, Michigan. (Photo by Gregory Shamus/Getty Images) Getty

ANN ARBOR, MICHIGAN - SEPTEMBER 26: Bryce Underwood #19 of the Michigan Wolverines looks to throw a first half pass against the Iowa Hawkeyes at Michigan Stadium on September 26, 2026 in Ann Arbor, Michigan. (Photo by Gregory Shamus/Getty Images) Getty

Bryce Underwood was supposed to be a Michigan Wolverines legend.

When they landed him in a massive flip in the Class of 2025, Underwood was the top recruit in the country. He was a Michigan kid. Underwood was meant to become a quarterback superstar in Ann Arbor.

Less than two full seasons have passed, and Underwood didn't get anywhere near the hype.

In fact, he's not even on the field right now.

Fans turning on the rivalry game between Michigan and Michigan State on Saturday, Nov. 7 are seeing Tommy Carr at quarterback, not Underwood.

It's not breaking news, but it's worth revisiting what happened.

GettyANN ARBOR, MICHIGAN – OCTOBER 24: Tommy Carr #14 of the Michigan Wolverines with Bryce Underwood #19 after a 13-6 overtime loss at Michigan Stadium on October 24, 2026 in Ann Arbor, Michigan. (Photo by Gregory Shamus/Getty Images)

Michigan benched Bryce Underwood midway through their game on Oct. 17.

The next week, the Wolverines gave Carr the start instead of Underwood.

Carr is now starting his third game for Michigan as they take on the in-state rival Spartans.

GettyANN ARBOR, MICHIGAN – OCTOBER 24: Tommy Carr #14 of the Michigan Wolverines with Bryce Underwood #19 after a 13-6 overtime loss at Michigan Stadium on October 24, 2026 in Ann Arbor, Michigan. (Photo by Gregory Shamus/Getty Images)

Carr is playing because Michigan head coach Kyle Whittingham continues to give him opportunities after the bold decision to bench Underwood.

The younger brother of Notre Dame's QB CJ Carr, Tommy Carr had his best game last week when he took on Rutgers.

Against the Scarlet Knights, Carr was reportedly battling the flu but still completed 22-of-27 passes for 266 yards and scored three total touchdowns, two on the ground and one through the air. Michigan won, 28-21.

"We know it's only one game, but if you project that one game out through the season, he'd be the best quarterback in the country, statistically," Whittingham told reporters in the lead-up to the Michigan State game.

GettyANN ARBOR, MICHIGAN – SEPTEMBER 26: Bryce Underwood #19 of the Michigan Wolverines looks to throw a first half pass against the Iowa Hawkeyes at Michigan Stadium on September 26, 2026 in Ann Arbor, Michigan. (Photo by Gregory Shamus/Getty Images)

One issue for Underwood was simply that he wasn't recruited by Michigan's current coaching staff. Whittingham didn't have the same ties to him.

That likely made it a bit easier for Whittingham to bench Underwood.

Now, Underwood might consider a transfer. If he doesn't think he'll start at Michigan next year, that may be his only logical path forward.

Underwood going into the transfer portal would be a bummer of an ending for something that once seemed so exciting for Michigan.

This article was originally published on HEAVY

#2835. What the Packers’ roster could look like vs. the Patriots

The Green Bay Packers only made one roster move on Saturday, bringing up practice squad tight end Drake Dabney to the game-day roster for action against the New England Patriots. As always, the moves that the Packers make, and don't make, give us hints at what the roster is expected to look like on Sunday. Let's get into the information we have now.

Four players have already been ruled out with injuries, bringing the Packers' 53-man active roster to 49. The players out are tight end Josh Whyle (concussion), offensive lineman Travis Glover (knee), offensive lineman Sean Rhyan (ankle) and linebacker Zaire Franklin (shin).

The Packers lost Whyle in-game against the Carolina Panthers, carrying only three tight ends on game day and scratching Jonnu Smith for the first time this season, while Glover, Rhyan and Franklin did not play.

This season, the Packers have seemingly had no interest in carrying four running backs on the game-day roster. The same can be said about a ninth offensive lineman, despite the team consistently keeping 11 on the active roster.

If you add in those scratches, Green Bay's active roster drops down to 47 players. With the addition of Dabney, who is expected to replace the special teams snaps of Whyle (the only real special teams contributor on offense who isn't a receiver), that gets us to a 48-man gameday roster. It's pretty tidy.

The players listed as questionable going into the weekend were running back MarShawn Lloyd (knee), cornerback Keisean Nixon (groin) and running back Chris Brooks (ankle). Brooks isn't on the active roster yet, as he's going through his practice window after spending time on the injured reserve. If he were going to play on Sunday, you would have seen an activation today.

Nixon is highly likely to play, as the Packers don't really have a replacement for him on the roster, as it stands currently. If he were going to miss this game, we probably would have seen a second call-up from the practice squad on Saturday. Lloyd may or may not play. If he can't go, the team will displace him with Kaleb Johnson, Green Bay's fourth running back, who has been a healthy scratch since Josh Jacobs' return to the team.

Here's what the team will probably look like on Sunday (with the biggest questions being which offensive lineman gets healthy-scratched and if Lloyd can go):

WR: Christian Watson, Matthew Golden, Savion Williams, Bo Melton, J. Michael Sturdivant and Coleman Owen

TE: Tucker Kraft, Mark Redman, Jonnu Smith and Drake Dabney

OL: 8 players up, 3 scratched (two for sure being Sean Rhyan and Travis Glover)

Most likely: Jordan Morgan, Aaron Banks, John Williams, Laken Tomlinson, Anthony Belton, Kevin Zeitler, Mekhi Becton Sr. and Jager Burton active; Donnovan Jennings a healthy scratch, along with Rhyan and Glover being out with injury.

DL: Karl Brooks, Devonte Wyatt, Chris McClellan, Jordon Riley and Anthony Campbell

ED: Lukas Van Ness, Barryn Sorrell, Brenton Cox Jr. and Dani Dennis-Sutton

LB: Isaiah McDuffie, Ty'Ron Hopper, Nick Niemann and Kristian Welch

CB: Keisean Nixon, Brandon Cisse, Carrington Valentine, Benjamin St-Juste and Kamal Hadden

S: Xavier McKinney, Evan Williams, Javon Bullard, Kitan Oladapo

That's your 48-man roster against the Patriots.

The Saints, on the last day before their first game since Week 7, have made a change.

#2833. Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30

Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers remain the Eastern Conference's sole leader.

They earned a 112-106 road win over the Pistons on Wednesday night to move to 8-1 on the season and stay at the top of the standings. Detroit fell to 6-2.

The Sixers last started 8-1 in the 2023-24 season, which was Nick Nurse's first as the team's head coach.

Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 30 points and pulled down seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid had 26 points and a season-best 15 rebounds. LeBron James posted 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. Tyrese Maxey added 19 points and seven assists.

The Pistons' leading scorers were Duncan Robinson with 27 points and Cade Cunningham with 26.

The Sixers were down Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pistons' injured players included Jalen Duren (right hamstring strain) and Isaiah Joe (right hip contusion).

Nurse told reporters in Detroit he thinks "it'll be another 10 to 12 days before they kind of clear (Barlow) for getting on the court to see where he is," according to Sixers Wire's Ky Carlin.

The Sixers will visit the Cavaliers on Friday night. Here are observations on their victory over Detroit:

Embiid assisted the Sixers' first basket with a sharp pass out of a double team. He saw Brown's cut along the baseline and dished to him for a layup.

Detroit ran several double drag screen actions early, forcing Embiid to defend on the perimeter. The Pistons started 7 for 11 from the floor and took a 17-11 edge on a Robinson three-pointer.

The Sixers then made a 10-0 run and Embiid was right in the middle of it. He converted a put-back layup after a pretty high-low feed from James. Embiid hit two foul shots to give the Sixers the lead. The team raised its defensive level and Detroit's offense grew frigid.

Embiid was very good on the glass in the first half, grabbing eight rebounds. The Sixers had an excellent rebounding game as a team, limiting the Pistons to four offensive boards over 48 minutes.

Embiid didn't break out of his early-season three-point shooting slump, going 0 for 3. He's 3 for 26 (11.5 percent) on the season and hasn't made a three since the Sixers' second game.

Late in the second quarter, Embiid got into a dead-ball dust-up with Tolu Smith. After an officials' review, Embiid was assessed a technical foul for "initiating" the altercation.

Playing on his 26th birthday, Maxey missed his first four field goals and was scoreless until he hit a floater with 31.5 seconds left in the first quarter.

Brown picked up the scoring slack, tallying 10 points in the first on 4-for-6 shooting and hitting a deep, buzzer-beating three to put the Sixers up 29-21. He produced at all three levels, chose smart spots to attack and looked comfortable in the rhythm of the Sixers' offense.

Meanwhile, James did his usuall stellar work as a passer. He assisted two Adem Bona slams in the second quarter and constantly spotted open teammates. Nurse rewarded Bona's strong opening stint, playing him for a lengthy stretch.

Brown swished a corner triple to extend the Sixers' lead to 48-31. With a 3-for-5 night, Brown is at 45.7 percent from long distance so far this season. It wouldn't be surprising at all if Brown's three-point shooting improved from last season's 34.7 percent clip. With such an abundance of star power, the Sixers' hope will be that everyone gets regular open looks beyond the arc throughout the year.

As for Maxey, he sat with 3:26 left in the second quarter after being whistled for his third foul. Even with a mere two points from Maxey, the Sixers entered halftime holding a 14-point advantage.

The Pistons played better basketball to begin the third quarter.

Following a John Collins alley-oop jam, Nurse called timeout with his team's lead down to single digits. The Pistons' offense sustained some success, but the Sixers prevented a sustained run.

Embiid played an especially physical, aggressive third quarter. He tipped in his own miss and Maxey then sunk a three to make the Sixers' lead 84-68. They were up as many as 17 points in the third.

Embiid sat out the fourth quarter Monday night for the Sixers' win over the Bulls and said he was on a minutes restriction (25) because of his right foot plantar fasciitis. He played the fourth period against the Pistons and logged 33 minutes. Bona went back to the locker room early in the fourth. Nurse told reporters postgame that Bona had "neck soreness."

Detroit did not let the Sixers coast to victory.

Robinson drained a three. Cunningham made a pair of free throws and drove in for a layup that cut the Pistons' deficit to 106-102.

The Sixers halted the Pistons' run before anything disastrous was on the table. Brown blocked a Cunningham three-point try, Embiid knocked down two foul shots, and the East's No. 1 early-season team improved to 4-0 on the road.

#2831. Joe Burrow can become third quarterback with four or more TD passes in four straight games

Bengals quarterback Joe Burrow had seven touchdown passes in his team’s first four games of the season. In the last three, he has had 13.

With four against the Dolphins, four against the Ravens, and five against the Titans, Burrow has the chance to become only the third quarterback in NFL history to throw four or more touchdown passes in four straight games.

Via NBC Sports research, the two others were Colts quarterback Peyton Manning in 2004 and Dolphins quarterback Dan Marino in 1984. Both won the league MVP award in their seasons of four straight four-or-more touchdown games.

Burrow’s odds of winning the MVP for 2026 are currently at +475, behind only Josh Allen and Brock Purdy.

Burrow currently leads the league with 20 touchdown passes. And the Bengals, after starting 2-2, are now 5-2.

They face the 3-5 Falcons on Sunday morning in Madrid at 9:30 a.m. ET on Sunday.
