#2829. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2828. Instant Recap: Michigan State Spartans Takes Down Michigan, 15-6

From early on in this game, and as many people already knew, it was clear that this was a game between two not good teams. Unfortunately for us Spartan fans, we have had a couple unlucky breaks while tonight's opponent has had some good fortune in their season. Watching the two teams in the first half, one would imagine their records were closer than the reality.

Michigan State got the ball first and moved the chains one time before punting.

On their first defensive stand, Michigan State got a turnover when safety Devin Vaught came down with a pick after a pass went off an um receiver's finger tips.

Starting a yard inside um territory, the Spartans reached 4th & 2 on the first series. Coach Fitzgerald decided to be aggressive, and the play call was for a deep shot. Personally, I would have rather punted here to try pinning an unimpressive offense deep. Predictably, the deep shot did not pay off. Receiver Bryson Williams had a chance at it but dropped the pass.

The two teams traded punts on their next possessions, totaling 17 yards of offense between them. On um's ensuing drive, they had their best drive of the half, moving 71 yards in 13 plays over six and a half minutes. They reached the MSU 7, but a 3rd & 1 run lost a yard and brought out the field goal unit. The kick went off the near side upright and bounced out, keeping the game with no points on the board.

Again, the two teams traded punts. Getting the ball right at the 50, the Spartans marched all the way to the 3-yard line before having to settle for a field goal, which Liam Boyd made. Just like that, the Spartans were ahead.

MSU then forced a 3-and-out. Chrishon McCray muffed the punt reception but retrieved the fumble, and MSU took over at their own 44. MSU picked up a couple first downs, the second on a 35-yard pass to McCray that got the Spartans down to the 13. A bogus offensive pass interference penalty in the endzone pushed MSU back near the 20 and eventually led to another FG try, which Boyd comfortably made.

There was only time for a kneel-down after the kickoff, and MSU took a 6-0 lead to the break. Our defense only gave up 78 total yards through two quarters.

MSU started the second half on defense, and after giving up a first down on the first play, forced a punt. Unfortunately for us, that punt was allowed to land and it flatlined at the 1-yard line, pinning Alessio Milivojevic and the MSU offense against the goalline.

Starting from the 1-yard line, Michigan State had, perhaps, their most impressive drive since week 2. The Spartans held the ball for 10:41 of game clock, ran 17 plays, and picked up 6 first downs. There were passes, runs, wildcats, and overall dominance from MSU. After reaching the um 12, the offense finally came to a halt with a run for a loss and then a sack. Liam Boyd came on and hit his 3rd field goal, this one from 38 yards, to put MSU up 9-0.

On um's ensuing drive, the referees started trying to help the home team. After it looked like MSU was going to force a 3-and-out, a penalty was called for "illegal substitution" because an MSU defender had the heel of his shoe still on the field when the ball was snapped. So um got another crack at a 3rd down play, on which MSU was called for pass interference (after a called reception was overturned). On the next play, um ran the ball through the middle of the Spartan defense, but Lafaele punched the ball out late and Nikai Martinez recovered the loose ball. Of course, after the play, MSU was penalized 15 yards for celebrating.

After a 28-yard catch-and-run by Chrishon McCray, MSU went backwards for a few plays and ended up punting, but Rhys Dakin pinned um at their own 7 with 12:01 to play.

After giving up a first down to start the drive, the Spartan defense then tackled um for a loss of a yard on a running play, followed that up with a sack by Derrick Simmons, and then got an interception from NiJhay Burt on a deep shot at the MSU 38.

Back on offense, the highlight of the drive was a 36-yard play to Bryson Williams. The drive lasted 10 plays and over 5 minutes of game clock, and ended in Boyd's fourth field goal to put MSU up 12-0.

With just over 5 minutes left, um needed to do something quick. Instead, they held the ball for two plays, fumbling at their 37-yard line after a catch. NiJhay Burt forced and recovered the fumble.

The Spartans ran the ball three times, then brought Boyd on to hit a 49-yard kick for a 15-0 lead.

The MSU defense then forced a turnover on downs on the first series of um's possession… but a pass interference gave um an extra life. After two incompletions and an 11-yard sack, MSU surrendered a 22-yard pass on 4th down. An 11-yard run moved the chains again for um. Two plays later, they hit a 31-yard pass to the 1, and then scored a play later to ruin the Spartan shutout. Now trailing 15-6, um elected to go for two, but Kenny Soares shut that down with an amazing spin move to get home for the sack.

The kickoff was an obvious onside attempt, but the ball only went 6 yards before hitting an um player, giving MSU the ball. Three kneeldowns later, it was game over and VICTORY FOR MSU!!!!

Sure, it would have been even sweeter to get the shutout, but this was still a dominant win against our rival in a game no one, including myself and many here among TOC Nation, was really giving us much of a chance to win. For the game, MSU outgained um 298-192 and controlled the clock with over 38 minutes of possession. The defense created four turnovers. As Pat Fitzgerald said after the game, "Paul Bunyan is coming home to East Lansing!"

While it can still be said that this was a game between two not good teams, it is clear that um is much more not good than MSU.

#2824. Miami’s game-tying touchdown denied by refs in controversial call during loss to Notre Dame

No. 1 Miami vs. No. 11 Notre Dame might have been the college football game of the year in South Bend on Saturday night. The Fighting Irish entered needing a signature win to get back in the good graces of the College Football Playoff Committee, and also seeking revenge for a season-opening loss to Miami last year that kept ND out of the playoff.

Notre Dame took control early with a 24-7 lead, but the Hurricanes would mount a comeback. Heisman Trophy candidate Darian Mensah put Miami in position to tie the game late in the fourth quarter, but a controversial call kept the Hurricanes out of the end zone and ultimately sealed their fate.

Notre Dame beat Miami, 31-24, to bolster its resume ahead of the playoff. It appeared Miami had briefly tied the game with under four minutes remaining when Mensah found tight end Elija Lofton in the corner of the end zone on fourth down, but the refs ruled that he didn't complete the catch, which gave Notre Dame the ball on a turnover on downs.

Loften caught the ball, and got both feet in-bounds, but the refs ruled that he did not perform an "act common to the game," which essentially means he didn't complete the catch before the Notre Dame defender knocked the ball away. Watch the play here.

In college football, a catch requires firm control, a body part down in bounds, and control long enough to perform an act common to the game. Lofton clearly had control. Was the play already over when Notre Dame defensive back Luke Talich stripped it?

The play was reviewed the refs affirmed their initial call of no catch. Notre Dame went on to win. Miami suffered its first loss of the season.

Do you think the refs got it right? That looks like a catch to me. Lofton stepped out of bounds and then the ball was knocked loss. Shouldn't that step count as a common act? Let us know what you think in the comments.

#2823. What we learned as Warriors' streak reaches seven games with OT win vs. Magic

What we learned as Warriors' streak reaches seven games with OT win vs. Magic originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SAN FRANCISCO – Winning six straight games is hard enough. Extending their win streak to seven straight looked like a walk in the park for the Warriors on Saturday night, until it wasn't.

The Warriors started off extremely strong, just to watch a 16-point lead fade away. Contenders need to rise to the challenge and overcome adversity, and that's what the Warriors did in a 126-119 overtime win over the Orlando Magic at Chase Center.

Beating the Magic gave the Warriors their first seven-game win streak without Jimmy Butler since January/February of their 2022 NBA championship season.

Steph Curry was incredible in the fourth quarter and overtime, as was rookie Yaxel Lendeborg.

The elder Curry scored a game-high 41 points on 13-of-19 shooting, going 5 of 10 from 3-point range and 10 of 10 at the free-throw line. Lendeborg added 23 points on 9-of-15 shooting and had eight rebounds.

A revitalized Strength In Numbers bench continued to be huge for the Warriors, outscoring the Magic 42 to 31.

There will be a lot to clean watching the film, but the Warriors will take a gritty win in the end.

Here are three takeaways from the Warriors improving to 7-3 on the season.

Winning time is where superstars are made. There's nobody in the building that doubts Curry in the clutch. But every hero needs a co-star alongside him. For Curry, that's a rookie wearing a cape.

The fourth quarter was won on the backs of a 38-year-old Curry and a 24-year-old Lendeborg in his 10th career game.

In the first three quarters, Lendeborg scored seven points and was a bit of an afterthought. Then in the fourth quarter, Lendeborg utilized his size and skill set to score nine huge points on a perfect 4-of-4 shooting. But this was another Steph Show for everyone to sit back and enjoy his 14 fourth-quarter points.

Between the two, Curry and Lendeborg combined to score 23 points in the fourth quarter on 8-of-9 shooting. Curry was on the bench when overtime began, coming in at the 3:48 mark with the Warriors ahead by two points. He and Lendeborg scored 12 of the Warriors' 16 points in overtime to claim victory.

Winning the margins is a major talking point of Warriors coach Steve Kerr to have success. His team needs to take care of the ball, focus on hitting singles instead of home runs and win the possession battle. The Warriors didn't execute those key parts of the game against a shorthanded Magic team that seemed to grab every loose ball.

Let's start with turnovers. As the first quarter crept to a close, the Warriors began to get loose with the ball. And the theme continued in the wrong ways for the home team. By halftime, even with the Warriors enjoying a 16-point lead, they already had given up 10 turnovers for 14 points.

As the Warriors' lead evaporated in the third quarter, they kept counting turnovers and were up to 14 for 18 points going into the fourth. But the Warriors only had one turnover between the fourth quarter and overtime, giving the ball away a total of 19 times for 26 points.

Another major area of concern was on the offensive glass. The Magic continued to keep possessions alive, and it finally caught up to the Warriors. While the Warriors came down with a lowly three offensive rebounds, the Magic secured a ridiculous 24, which helped lead to 33 second-chance points compared to the Warriors' two.

However he can, Gui Santos wants to help the Warriors win games. He was pushing through a left knee injury that held him back, and finally had to be told to take a seat. Santos missed the previous two games, both Warriors wins, before returning Saturday night.

He was back in the starting lineup, but couldn't give the Warriors much. At all.

Santos played just 11-plus minutes and didn't have much of an impact. Playing less than he usually would, Santos had more turnovers (two) than shots made (one). That's in a game where he has just one rebound and no assists.

Defensively, Santos didn't have much burst either. It's clear he's less than 100 percent, and the Warriors will need a much better version of him soon. Santos finished as a minus-4 with three points and didn't leave the bench in the fourth quarter or overtime.

#2821. Three things I’m looking at in the New York Jets vs. Kansas City Chiefs Game

We're back ready to watch our New York Jets play against the Kansas City Chiefs. For the third straight week, the Jets are facing a team that's coming off consecutive losses and looking to "get right" against Gang Green. Miami and Las Vegas were able to accomplish that feat and it seems like Kansas City should theoretically have no problem doing the same. The Chiefs started the season 5-0 and although they've been inconsistent, they are coached by one of the greatest coaches in history, have a great defense and a first ballot hall of famer at QB.

It'll be quite the tall task for the Jets to upset the Chiefs. While the Jets were actually the favorites going into the last two weeks at home, they are a 9.5-point underdog as they enter Arrowhead Stadium. It's tied with the Raiders-Niners game for the largest spread in the NFL this week so that should help paint the picture of how tough this will be for the Jets to overcome. Still, anything is possible and this year's Jets have firepower! Let's take a look:

I usually pride myself on discussing the "lesser-talked" items that don't get mainstream media attention, but the elephant in the Jets' RB room is too massive to ignore. The entire fanbase and media have completely turned on Breece Hall (again) and have spent the last month hypothetically trading him all over the league. As the sole human being left who still wants him to spend his entire 20's as a Jet, this game seems like a real pivotal moment for that dream I have.

Let's make no mistake about it: Braelon Allen has been absolutely incredible these last four weeks. He has arguably been the best rusher in the entire NFL during this time, averaging over 100 yards per game and scoring the game-winning touchdown against the Browns. He's proven to be a valuable rushing asset, which was very much up for debate before he suited up against the Bears this season.

The question is, is there enough pie for multiple running backs to eat? Plenty of teams thrive with duos that eat consistently on a weekly basis. The Rams' Kyren Williams and Blake Corum, the Bears' D'Andre Swift and Kyle Monangai, and Broncos' J.K. Dobbins and R.J. Harvey are some quick examples. Even the Raiders who just beat the Jets last week had Mike Washington take 25 carries and Ashton Jeanty still finish the game with 70+ scrimmage yards.

It's up to Frank Reich to deploy these two Killer Bs in a way that maximizes both players' talent, and I think it's easier to do than my colleagues and fellow Jets content creators have claimed. I think there is plenty to go around and wholeheartedly believe it'd be a grave mistake to trade Breece Hall. Of course, conventional wisdom says to keep Braelon running his 17+ carries per game and then utilize Breece's route running and hands to be a weapon in the pass game.

I agree with that general philosophy, but would still value Breece's rushing ability. His issues have been between the tackles but he's explosive in space and I'd utilize those young tackles to deploy outside runs to get Breece going. Luckily, this game will be away, so the crowd won't be a factor – I believe the impatient short-sighted fans have already made their decision so if Hall were to start slow he'd hear his own fans booing him. At Arrowhead, this is a nonfactor, he has the freedom to get himself involved without hearing from the peanut gallery and a chance to prove they can be a duo for years to come.

What does success look like? The Chiefs have allowed 100+ scrimmage to opposing RB1s in three of the last four weeks. That one exception is the Chargers, who are still winless and have been horrible all year. I'm looking for 100+ scrimmage yards for RB1 and 50+ scrimmage yards for RB2. Now, who is RB1 and RB2? That's up for Frank Reich, Breece Hall, and Braelon Allen to decide.

I think we can all agree that Aaron Glenn's superpowers of defensive play-calling are the worst 'powers' out there and that the Jets defense has been an issue. It looks like things are going to get bad to worse because the Chiefs are looking to get their high-powered offense back on track. They were averaging 28 points and undefeated until this two-game skid hit and they scored 27 points combined in these last two.

Now they're facing a Jets team that has allowed 30.6(!) points per game during this three game losing streak. They've especially been leaky to the the running back position, which is horrible news when you consider Kenneth Walker is having a career year. Only Jahmyr Gibbs has more rushing yards per game and only Jaylen Warren has more yards per carry.

They also having a rookie running back Emmett Johnson who just caught five passes for 128(!) yards last week and a touchdown last week. Will this running back duo show us what a real duo looks like and run wild on the porous Jets defense this week? What adjustments can Glenn make, what players can step up, or will it just be "Jets beating Jets" like Minkah Fitzpatrick said last week?

Garrett Wilson is set to miss his first game of the season with a concussion, and while that normally meant complete despair for the offense in the past, there are lots of options to step up this Sunday. Of course Adonai Mitchell and Omar Cooper Jr. seem like the natural choices, but there are cases to be made for Mason Taylor, Kenyon Sadiq and Isaiah Williams. Even the aforementioned Breece Hall can get a lot of looks in the pass game.

Wilson had 12 targets last week. You may be thinking "how do we replace all those targets?" but in the weeks prior you may recall that his targets have been an issue. Geno has had no problem spreading the ball around all season so I'd imagine this week will be more of the same. The most interesting thing to me is seeing who garners the most attention from the Chiefs defense.

If you were Steve Spagnuolo what would you do? Mitchell is coming off a 142-yard game but Omar Cooper Jr. had consecutive 100 yard games before that. With attention and potential double teams occurring on the outside is this a game for the tight ends to finally feast? Sadiq still only has 5 catches for 43 scoreless yards in the five games since his 100-yard breakout performance against the Lions. Hopefully Frank Reich and Geno Smith can keep the offensive momentum rolling without their best WR on the field. Let's go Jets!

#2819. Everett outlasts Conemaugh Township in District 5-2A championship

RICHLAND TOWNSHIP — Conemaugh Township eyed its second upset of the week when it took on to top-seeded Everett in the District 5 Class 2A volleyball championship, Saturday, Nov. 7, at Pitt-Johnstown.

Like its semifinal final contest against Windber, the title tilt went the distance. Unfortunately, Conemaugh Township could not complete the rally, as the Warriors earned a 23-25, 25-23, 25-19, 11-25, 15-12 victory.

"You can't wait until the third and fourth game to put the pedal to the metal," said Conemaugh Township coach Laura Swank. "The nerves got to some of my players. Once we settled in, we had a battle. Everett, they're a phenomenal team. They're very scrappy. They don't give up."

Champs again Berlin Brothersvalley bests Meyersdale for 4th straight D5-1A title

Ones to watch Berlin bids for 4th straight D5 volleyball title, Class 2A wide open

There were nine ties in the first set. After an 8-1 run by the Warriors, Conemaugh Township went on a 7-0 surge for a 22-21 advantage. Conemaugh Township closed the set, tallying 3 of the final 4 points, taking a 1-0 lead in the match.

The second set was another battle. Trailing 17-12, Conemaugh Township notched five straight points to even things. There were three more ties from that point, but Everett came up with some big kills to close the set and tie the match at a set apiece.

It was a trio of Everett runs that led to a triumph in the third set. Trailing 7-2, the Warriors tallied four straight and were down by only a point. Down 10-8, Everett notched five in a row for its first lead in the set. The Warriors put the set out of reach with an 8-1 run in the latter stages of the set.

With its season hanging in the balance, Conemaugh Township dominated the fourth set. It never trailed once, forcing a fifth and deciding set.

However, Everett responded in the fifth set. The defending champs did not trail. Tied at 10 apiece, the Warriors rattled off the next three points and recorded back-to-back kills to claim the championship.

Conemaugh Township was led by junior Sarah Swank's 34 kills and 17 digs, while senior Danika Black provided nine blocks, nine digs and seven kills. Fellow senior Averie Seibert dished out 34 assists to go along with nine digs, while Mary Matera added 23 assists and 11 digs. Quinn Moser and Mya Nemeth combined for 36 digs, and sophomore Cora Oswalt delivered 14 blocks and 11 kills.

"I'm very proud of them," Coach Swank said. "We have heart and they will battle to the end. Nobody quit. They put their best effort forward and gave it their all."

Somerset avenged a regular season loss to Central Mountain with a 25-20, 25-14, 25-17 victory in the District 5-6-8 Class 4A championship game at Mount Aloysius College.

Kaycee Stairs recorded 17 kills and three blocks, while Thressa Ledney tallied eight kills, six aces and six digs for the Golden Eagles. Elly Fletcher provided 30 assists, while Ella Thompson notched 11 digs, seven kills and four aces. Kayla Piccola chipped in six digs.

Somerset visits District 4 champion Athens in the first round of the PIAA playoffs at 5 p.m., Wednesday, Nov. 11.

#2816. Top NFL draft prospect Drew Mestemaker has huge day with Jets GM Darren Mougey watching

Drew Mestemaker continues to make his case to move up draft boards.

It's possible his potential future boss was in the building to see his huge Saturday for Oklahoma State.

The Cowboys quarterback accounted for five touchdowns — four passing and one receiving — as Oklahoma State creamed Kansas State 66-28. And in the building to see the loud performance at Bill Snyder Family Stadium was Jets general manager Darren Mougey, who was spotted talking with Oklahoma State offensive coordinator Sean Brophy before kickoff.

Right now, the Jets, who are conceivably in the market to draft a quarterback in 2027 at 2-6, have the No. 4 overall pick (via the Colts from the Sauce Gardner trade), the sixth pick and the 10th pick from the Cowboys, according to Tankathon.

While a lot of the preseason talk and punditry focused on Texas' Arch Manning, Mestemaker has made the case to be the top quarterback selected in next year's draft. In ESPN draft guru Mel Kiper Jr.'s latest big board, he ranks Mestemaker as the No. 2 overall prospect and top quarterback in 2027.

Pro Football Focus also has him as the No. 1 quarterback taken in the draft.

Mestemaker threw for 4,379 yards last season and has eclipsed the 2,600-yard passing mark this year. He already has six games this season in which he's completed at least three touchdown passes, throwing for five scores twice.

Oklahoma State Cowboys quarterback Drew Mestemaker runs for a touchdown against the Kansas State Wildcats in Manhattan, Kan., Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026. USA TODAY Network via Reuters Connect

The quarterback, though, was pretty amped to catch a 5-yard touchdown in the win Saturday.

"I gotta say catching a touchdown," Mestemaker told TNT Sports when asked about what the most gratifying type of touchdown of his career has been. "That's the first of my career. That was definitely the most fun touchdown of the day."

Whether it's the Jets or another team, Mestemaker could prove to be a game-changer with his arm skills.

#2815. Patriots activate Mack Hollins from IR

The Patriots won’t have A.J. Brown or Romeo Doubs on Sunday, but they will be getting another wide receiver back from injury against the Packers.

They have activated Mack Hollins from injured reserve. Hollins returned to practice this week after missing the last four games with a calf injury.

Hollins had 15 catches for 209 yards in four appearances before his injury. He had 46 catches for 550 yards and two touchdowns in his first season with the Patriots.

Brown will miss his second straight game with a knee injury and Doubs remains on injured reserve. Pop Douglas, Efton Chism, and Kyle Williams join Hollins as the available receivers on the 53-man roster for New England.

#2814. VfB Stuttgart coach Hoeneß reportedly highly rated by Manchester United

According to UK newspaper The Independent, Sebastian Hoeneß is apparently held in high reputation at Manchester United.

The 44-year-old is reportedly a candidate to succeed Michael Carrick, whose position as manager is under considerable pressure.

However, a transfer would be difficult for the Red Devils to implement given Hoeneß is still under contract with VfB Stuttgart until 2028.

As a result, it would also mean that tricky negotiations with the Bundesliga club from Baden-Württemberg would also be necessary.

Other replacements reportedly include Julian Nagelsmann, Didier Deschamps, Eddie Howe, Fabian Hürzeler and Antonio Conte.

Hoeneß has already been loosely linked with Real Madrid and Liverpool in the past, and now another major club seems to be joining the list.

#2813. Angels rumors: Infielder joins Zach Neto as potential offseason trade candidate

Angels rumors: Infielder joins Zach Neto as potential offseason trade candidate appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Zach Neto has already been floated as a potential trade chip for the Los Angeles Angels this offseason, and now another young infielder is being mentioned alongside him. With the franchise at a crossroads, first baseman Nolan Schanuel could also be on the move if the club decides to rebuild.

MLB.com's Rhett Bollinger noted that both players are firmly entrenched in the Angels' infield, but their futures could depend on which direction the team takes.

"The Angels have Zach Neto entrenched at shortstop and Nolan Schanuel at first base, but either could be a trade candidate if they decide to rebuild," Bollinger wrote.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

The Angels are coming off a brutal 2026 season in which they lost 100 games. On top of that, the organization is in the middle of a major transition. Arte Moreno agreed to sell the team to Kroenke Sports & Entertainment for a reported $4 billion, and the Angels are not expected to hire a new general manager or manager until the sale is approved.

Interim GM John Mozeliak is set to depart when his contract expires in December.

Neto has been at the center of trade speculation for months. The Boston Red Sox reportedly had strong interest in the 25-year-old shortstop ahead of the trade deadline, and MLB Network's Jon Morosi recently suggested the Angels should explore the market.

"This is the time to gauge interest in Zach Neto and Reid Detmers," Morosi said on MLB Network's "Hot Stove." "It's not about winning a World Series in 2027."

It is easy to see why teams would be interested. Neto slugged 27 home runs and drove in 77 runs in 157 games in 2026, and he has three years of team control remaining.

Schanuel, meanwhile, is 24 years old and coming off a solid campaign in which he hit .276 with seven home runs, 49 RBIs and a .736 OPS in 107 games. The former first-round pick has been a steady presence at first base since debuting just weeks after being drafted in 2023.

For now, any major moves will likely have to wait until the ownership change is finalized. But if the new regime chooses to tear things down, Neto and Schanuel could be two of the most intriguing names on the trade market.
